  189: export async function handleSubmitEvidence(deps: GuestArchiveHttpDeps, req: GuestArchiveHttpRequest<SubmitEvidenceInput>): Promise<GuestArchiveHttpResponse> {
  190:   return withErrorMapping(async () => {
  191:     requireToken(req); // Presence-checked for route symmetry/observability; resolution is by session cookie, not the path token.
  192:     // Pre-existing gap, found (not introduced) by Codex review round 1 of D-264 (Block 6, G02
  193:     // frontend): the session cookie (GUEST_SESSION_COOKIE_NAME) is a single global cookie per
  194:     // browser, never bound to/verified against THIS route's own `token` path segment. Two guest
  195:     // links open in two tabs of the same browser share one cookie jar - the second `session`
  196:     // mint overwrites the first tab's cookie, so the first tab's later `uploads` call resolves
  197:     // against the SECOND session/DocumentRequest, not its own. Real since D-146 (a single-link-
  198:     // at-a-time design); only now reachable via a real multi-tab UI. Fixing this means binding
  199:     // the session to its issuing token (or scoping the cookie name by token) - a session-identity
  200:     // design change that deserves its own scoping, not a rushed patch. Named in decisions-log
  201:     // D-264.
  202:     // ADR-0013 (D-265) implementation, Codex review round 1: a missing body used to throw a
  203:     // differentiated `ValidationError` (400 with detail) while an invalid one collapsed to the
  204:     // generic guest error — an anti-enumeration leak the schema-validation collapse below was
  205:     // meant to close but didn't cover. Both now collapse identically.
  206:     if (!req.body) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  207:     validateGuestSubmitSchema(req.body);
  208: 
  209:     const cookies = cookiesOf(req);
  210:     const sessionToken = cookies[GUEST_SESSION_COOKIE_NAME];
  211:     // A missing session cookie is itself a guest-auth failure mode (no session to resolve) —
  212:     // collapses into the same generic error as a malformed/expired/wrong-secret token, never a
  213:     // differentiated 400 that would tell a caller "you're just missing a cookie" (an oracle).
  214:     if (!sessionToken) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  215:     const csrfHeaderValue = req.headers?.[CSRF_HEADER_NAME] ?? req.headers?.["X-CSRF-Token"];
  216: 
  217:     const result = await deps.guestAccess.submitEvidence(
  218:       sessionToken,
  219:       { ip: req.sourceIp, csrfCookieValue: cookies[GUEST_CSRF_COOKIE_NAME], csrfHeaderValue },
  220:       req.body,
  221:     );
  222:     return { statusCode: 201, headers: baseHeaders(), body: result as unknown as Record<string, unknown> };
  223:   });
  224: }
  225: 
  226: const CONFIRM_UPLOAD_SCHEMA_ID = "https://expiration-tracker/schemas/api/docarchive-guest-confirm-upload-request.v1.json";
  227: 
  228: /** PATCH /document-archive/guest/document-requests/{token}/uploads — ADR-0013 (D-265). Same
  229:  * path as `handleSubmitEvidence` (a different HTTP verb, not a new CloudFront behavior — the
  230:  * existing guest-uploads CloudFront cache behavior already matches this exact path regardless
  231:  * of method), same session-cookie/CSRF discipline. */
  232: export async function handleConfirmUpload(deps: GuestArchiveHttpDeps, req: GuestArchiveHttpRequest<{ idempotencyKey: string }>): Promise<GuestArchiveHttpResponse> {
  233:   return withErrorMapping(async () => {
  234:     requireToken(req);
  235:     // Same anti-enumeration collapse as handleSubmitEvidence above — missing and invalid bodies
  236:     // must be indistinguishable to the caller.
  237:     if (!req.body) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  238:     try {
  239:       validateAgainstSchema(CONFIRM_UPLOAD_SCHEMA_ID, req.body);
  240:     } catch {
  241:       throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  242:     }
  243: 
  244:     const cookies = cookiesOf(req);
  245:     const sessionToken = cookies[GUEST_SESSION_COOKIE_NAME];
  246:     if (!sessionToken) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  247:     const csrfHeaderValue = req.headers?.[CSRF_HEADER_NAME] ?? req.headers?.["X-CSRF-Token"];
  248: 
  249:     const result = await deps.guestAccess.confirmUploadInFlight(
  250:       sessionToken,
  251:       { ip: req.sourceIp, csrfCookieValue: cookies[GUEST_CSRF_COOKIE_NAME], csrfHeaderValue },
  252:       req.body.idempotencyKey,
  253:     );
  254:     return { statusCode: 200, headers: baseHeaders(), body: result as unknown as Record<string, unknown> };
  255:   });
  256: }
FILE: src/modules/document-archive/domain/request-access-credential.ts
    1: /**
    2:  * RequestAccessCredential — D-143 Decision 4 (guest access, layer 1 of 3). Long-duration,
    3:  * revocable `selector.secret` pair granting an external (non-authenticated) party the right to
    4:  * view ONE `DocumentRequest` and, via `GuestSession`, eventually upload evidence for it. TTL
    5:  * equals the business Request's deadline (Decision 4: "TTL = prazo do Request de negócio") —
    6:  * never a fixed literal like the older subject-module `GuestTokenPointer`'s 14 days.
    7:  *
    8:  * Crypto (deliberate deviation from the design doc's illustrative "Argon2id" wording, traceable
    9:  * per D-143 Decision 4's E-014 research declaration — SIM, NIST SP 800-63B-4 §5.1.2): this
   10:  * codebase has ZERO Argon2id anywhere (verified by grep before writing this file). Argon2id's
   11:  * memory-hardness exists to slow an OFFLINE brute-force search against a LOW-entropy secret
   12:  * (a human-chosen password drawn from a small effective keyspace). The `secret` half of this
   13:  * credential is 256 bits of `randomBytes` — a high-entropy secret NIST SP 800-63B-4 §5.1.2
   14:  * classifies as not needing a memory-hard KDF at all; a plain keyed hash already makes offline
   15:  * guessing infeasible (2^256 search space), and Argon2id would only add real CPU/memory cost
   16:  * to every legitimate verification for no corresponding security gain. This module therefore
   17:  * reuses the exact HMAC-SHA256 + versioned pepper + `timingSafeEqual` + dummy-hash anti-timing
   18:  * pattern already established and battle-tested by `src/modules/subject/domain/guest-token.ts`
   19:  * (same architecture, same reasoning applies identically to this credential's secret shape) —
   20:  * not a new `argon2` dependency.
   21:  *
   22:  * Lookup via tenantless pointer item (`DOCARCHIVEGUEST#<selectorHash>`/`POINTER`) — another
   23:  * instance of the same documented tenantless-partition exception `guest-token.ts` names as its
   24:  * own "third exception" (after `IdentityMapping` and GSI3): the lookup happens BEFORE `tenantId`
   25:  * is known, so the PK cannot begin with `TENANT#<tenantId>`.
   26:  */
   27: import { createHmac, randomBytes, timingSafeEqual } from "node:crypto";
   28: import type { EntityKey } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/occ.js";
   29: 
   30: export interface RequestAccessCredential extends EntityKey {
   31:   SK: "POINTER";
   32:   entityType: "RequestAccessCredential";
   33:   selectorHash: string;
   34:   secretHash: string;
   35:   tenantId: string;
   36:   subjectId: string;
   37:   requirementId: string;
   38:   documentRequestId: string;
   39:   tokenVersion: number;
   40:   expiresAt: string;
   41:   /** DynamoDB TTL attribute (epoch seconds) — same D-047/D-048 lesson `GuestTokenPointer`
   42:    * documents: `expiresAt` alone is only ever read by application code, never triggers physical
   43:    * deletion on its own. */
   44:   purgeAfterTtl: number;
   45:   revokedAt?: string;
   46:   createdAt: string;
   47:   updatedAt: string;
   48:   version: number;
   49: }
   50: 
   51: export function requestAccessCredentialKey(selectorHash: string): { PK: string; SK: "POINTER" } {
   52:   return { PK: `DOCARCHIVEGUEST#${selectorHash}`, SK: "POINTER" };
   53: }
   54: 
   55: export function epochSecondsFromIso(iso: string): number {
   56:   return Math.floor(Date.parse(iso) / 1000);
   57: }
   58: 
   59: /** Pepper is injected from config/Secrets Manager by the composition root — this module only
   60:  * implements the hash/compare mechanics, same separation as `guest-token.ts`'s `GuestTokenCrypto`. */
   61: export interface RequestAccessCrypto {
   62:   hash(pepper: string, value: string): string;
   63: }
   64: 
   65: export const hmacRequestAccessCrypto: RequestAccessCrypto = {
   66:   hash(pepper: string, value: string): string {
   67:     return createHmac("sha256", pepper).update(value).digest("hex");
   68:   },
   69: };
   70: 
   71: export interface IssuedRequestAccessCredential {
   72:   /** Full `selector.secret` value embedded in the link sent to the external party — never
   73:    * persisted raw. */
   74:   token: string;
   75:   selector: string;
   76:   selectorHash: string;
   77:   secretHash: string;
   78: }
   79: 
   80: /** Generates a new high-entropy selector/secret pair (128/256 bits respectively — same sizing
   81:  * as `guest-token.ts`'s `issueGuestToken`). */
   82: export function issueRequestAccessCredential(pepper: string, crypto: RequestAccessCrypto = hmacRequestAccessCrypto): IssuedRequestAccessCredential {
   83:   const selector = randomBytes(16).toString("hex");
   84:   const secret = randomBytes(32).toString("hex");
   85:   return {
   86:     token: `${selector}.${secret}`,
   87:     selector,
   88:     selectorHash: crypto.hash(pepper, selector),
   89:     secretHash: crypto.hash(pepper, secret),
   90:   };
   91: }
   92: 
   93: export interface ParsedRequestAccessToken {
   94:   selector: string;
   95:   secret: string;
   96: }
   97: 
   98: /** Parse never throws — malformed input takes the same dummy-comparison path as every other
   99:  * failure mode (anti-enumeration). */
  100: export function parseRequestAccessToken(raw: string): ParsedRequestAccessToken | undefined {
  101:   const parts = raw.split(".");
  102:   if (parts.length !== 2) return undefined;
  103:   const [selector, secret] = parts;
  104:   if (!selector || !secret || !/^[a-f0-9]{32}$/.test(selector) || !/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/.test(secret)) return undefined;
  105:   return { selector, secret };
  106: }
  107: 
  108: /** `timingSafeEqual` comparison — never `===` on a value derived from a secret. */
  109: export function requestAccessSecretMatches(pepper: string, secret: string, expectedSecretHash: string, crypto: RequestAccessCrypto = hmacRequestAccessCrypto): boolean {
  110:   const actual = Buffer.from(crypto.hash(pepper, secret), "hex");
  111:   const expected = Buffer.from(expectedSecretHash, "hex");
  112:   if (actual.length !== expected.length) return false;
  113:   return timingSafeEqual(actual, expected);
  114: }

  255:     const selectorHash = hmacRequestAccessCrypto.hash(this.pepper, parsed.selector);
  256: 
  257:     // Rate limit consumed BEFORE lookup, by both the selector (computable even if no pointer
  258:     // exists) and IP — Decision 4's multidimensional requirement, applied at the earliest point
  259:     // an oracle could otherwise form (same discipline as GuestSubmissionService.resolveToken).
  260:     try {
  261:       await this.rateLimiter.consumeBoth({ requestKey: selectorHash, ip: requestContext.ip, limit: RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE, windowSeconds: RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS });
  262:     } catch {
  263:       throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  264:     }
  265: 
  266:     const pointer = await this.store.get<RequestAccessCredential>(requestAccessCredentialKey(selectorHash));
  267: 
  268:     // Dummy anti-timing path — even when no pointer exists, still compute a hash and a
  269:     // timingSafeEqual comparison so response time never depends on selector existence. The dummy
  270:     // hash's fixed prefix can never collide with a real secretHash (issueRequestAccessCredential
  271:     // never produces this literal input).
  272:     const targetSecretHash = pointer?.secretHash ?? hmacRequestAccessCrypto.hash(this.pepper, `dummy:${selectorHash}`);
  273:     const secretOk = requestAccessSecretMatches(this.pepper, parsed.secret, targetSecretHash);
  274:     if (!pointer || !secretOk) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  275: 
  276:     if (pointer.revokedAt) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  277:     if (pointer.expiresAt < this.now()) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  278: 
  279:     const request = await this.store.get<DocumentRequest>(documentRequestKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(pointer), pointer.subjectId, pointer.documentRequestId));
  280:     if (!request || !isDocumentRequestLive(request.status)) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  281:     // Re-validate against the live Request's deadline too (same "credential TTL OR deadline,
  282:     // whichever is sooner, re-checked at resolve time" discipline as GuestTokenPointer's D-037
  283:     // precedent) — catches a deadline edited after the credential was issued without reissuing it.
  284:     if (request.deadline && request.deadline < this.now()) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  285: 
  286:     if (request.status === "REQUESTED") await this.markOpened(request);
  287: 
  288:     const tenantId = authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(pointer);
  289:     const [subject, requirement] = await Promise.all([
  290:       this.store.get<EntityKey & { displayName?: string }>(trackedSubjectKeyForFence(tenantId, pointer.subjectId)),
  291:       this.store.get<EntityKey & { name?: string }>(requirementKey(tenantId, pointer.subjectId, pointer.requirementId)),
  292:     ]);
  293: 
  294:     return { credential: pointer, request, subjectDisplayName: subject?.displayName, requirementName: requirement?.name };
  295:   }
  296: 
  297:   /**
  298:    * Discovery route (item 6 of D-173's estado-final-consolidado.md, engineering-only slice —
  299:    * see D-19x for the full contradiction/pendency writeup): lets a guest who has a valid
  300:    * credential token discover which `DocumentTypeId`s are currently valid to submit, WITHOUT any
  301:    * `authorize()`/`RequestContext` — same token-resolution discipline as `resolveCredential()`,
  302:    * reused here rather than duplicated. Only ACTIVE types are ever returned (never DRAFT/
  303:    * DEPRECATED) — a guest has no legitimate reason to see a type it cannot successfully submit
  304:    * against, since D-184's transactional ConditionCheck would reject anything else anyway.
  305:    * Read-only, no side effect (does NOT call `markOpened` — that is `resolveCredential`'s own
  306:    * concern and this route can be polled/refreshed by a guest UI without mutating Request state).
  307:    */
  308:   async listActiveDocumentTypesForGuest(rawToken: string, requestContext: { ip: string }): Promise<GuestDocumentTypeSummary[]> {
  309:     const parsed = parseRequestAccessToken(rawToken);
  310:     if (!parsed) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  311: 
  312:     const selectorHash = hmacRequestAccessCrypto.hash(this.pepper, parsed.selector);
  313:     try {
  314:       await this.rateLimiter.consumeBoth({ requestKey: selectorHash, ip: requestContext.ip, limit: RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE, windowSeconds: RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS });
  315:     } catch {
  316:       throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  317:     }
  318: 
  319:     const pointer = await this.store.get<RequestAccessCredential>(requestAccessCredentialKey(selectorHash));
  320:     const targetSecretHash = pointer?.secretHash ?? hmacRequestAccessCrypto.hash(this.pepper, `dummy:${selectorHash}`);
  321:     const secretOk = requestAccessSecretMatches(this.pepper, parsed.secret, targetSecretHash);
  322:     if (!pointer || !secretOk) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  323:     if (pointer.revokedAt) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  324:     if (pointer.expiresAt < this.now()) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  325: 
  326:     const request = await this.store.get<DocumentRequest>(documentRequestKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(pointer), pointer.subjectId, pointer.documentRequestId));
  327:     if (!request || !isDocumentRequestLive(request.status)) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  328:     if (request.deadline && request.deadline < this.now()) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  329: 
  330:     // Same GSI1 DOCTYPESTATUS namespace as DocumentArchiveService.listDocumentTypes, read
  331:     // directly against the store (never through that authorize()-gated method — this whole
  332:     // module never calls authorize()/RequestContextResolver, see file header). Catalogs are
  333:     // small (a handful to low hundreds of types per tenant), so — unlike the internal
  334:     // paginated-by-design admin listing — this accumulates every page here: a guest-facing
  335:     // discovery list has no caller-driven cursor to hand back, and a hard page cap (`MAX_PAGES`)
  340:     for (let page = 0; page < MAX_PAGES; page++) {
  341:       const result = await this.store.queryIndexPage<DocumentType>({
  342:         indexName: "GSI1",
  343:         partitionKeyValue: `TENANT#${pointer.tenantId}#DOCTYPESTATUS#ACTIVE`,
  344:         exclusiveStartKey,
  345:       });
  346:       items.push(...result.items);
  347:       if (!result.lastEvaluatedKey) break;
  348:       exclusiveStartKey = result.lastEvaluatedKey;
  349:     }
  350: 
  351:     return items.map((item) => ({ documentTypeId: item.documentTypeId, displayName: item.displayName }));
  352:   }
  353: 
  354:   /** Layer 2: the ONLY way a GuestSession is minted — always an explicit call, never a side
  355:    * effect of `resolveCredential`. */
  356:   async startGuestSession(rawToken: string, requestContext: { ip: string }): Promise<StartGuestSessionResult> {
  357:     const resolved = await this.resolveCredential(rawToken, requestContext);
  358:     const issued = issueGuestSession(this.pepper);
  359:     const now = this.now();
  360:     const expiresAt = new Date(Date.parse(now) + GUEST_SESSION_TTL_SECONDS * 1000).toISOString();
  361:     const session: GuestSession = {
  362:       ...guestSessionKey(issued.selectorHash),
  363:       entityType: "GuestSession",
  364:       selectorHash: issued.selectorHash,
  365:       secretHash: issued.secretHash,
  366:       tenantId: resolved.credential.tenantId,
  367:       subjectId: resolved.credential.subjectId,
  368:       requirementId: resolved.credential.requirementId,
  369:       documentRequestId: resolved.credential.documentRequestId,
  370:       credentialSelectorHash: resolved.credential.selectorHash,
  371:       csrfTokenHash: issued.csrfTokenHash,
  372:       expiresAt,
  373:       purgeAfterTtl: epochSecondsFromIso(expiresAt),
  374:       createdAt: now,
  375:       updatedAt: now,
  376:       version: 1,
  377:     };
  378:     const created = await this.store.putIfAbsent(session);
  379:     if (!created) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError(); // astronomically unlikely selector collision.
  380:     return { session: issued, expiresAt, subjectDisplayName: resolved.subjectDisplayName, requirementName: resolved.requirementName };
  381:   }
  382: 
  383:   /** Resolves a GuestSession token — same parse/rate-limit/lookup/dummy-compare/expiry
  384:    * discipline as `resolveCredential`, over the session's own tenantless namespace. */
  385:   private async resolveSession(rawSessionToken: string, requestContext: { ip: string }): Promise<ResolvedSession> {
  386:     const parsed = parseGuestSessionToken(rawSessionToken);
  387:     if (!parsed) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  388: 
  389:     const selectorHash = hmacGuestSessionCrypto.hash(this.pepper, parsed.selector);
  390:     try {
  391:       await this.rateLimiter.consumeBoth({ requestKey: selectorHash, ip: requestContext.ip, limit: RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE, windowSeconds: RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS });
  392:     } catch {
  393:       throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  394:     }
  395: 
  396:     const pointer = await this.store.get<GuestSession>(guestSessionKey(selectorHash));
  397:     const targetSecretHash = pointer?.secretHash ?? hmacGuestSessionCrypto.hash(this.pepper, `dummy:${selectorHash}`);
  398:     const secretOk = guestSessionSecretMatches(this.pepper, parsed.secret, targetSecretHash);
  399:     if (!pointer || !secretOk) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  400:     if (pointer.expiresAt < this.now()) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  401: 
  402:     const request = await this.store.get<DocumentRequest>(documentRequestKey(authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(pointer), pointer.subjectId, pointer.documentRequestId));
  403:     if (!request || !isDocumentRequestLive(request.status)) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  404: 
  405:     return { session: pointer, request };
  406:   }
  407: 
  408:   /** Double-submit CSRF check shared by `submitEvidence()`/`confirmUploadInFlight()` (Codex
  409:    * review round 1, D-265 implementation: this was copy-pasted between the two before, real
  410:    * drift risk the ADR's own "reused, not duplicated" intent named). Cookie and header must both
  411:    * be present AND equal AND match the session's own stored hash (defends both "no cookie at
  412:    * all" and "attacker can set an arbitrary matching pair but doesn't know the real session's
  413:    * csrfToken" — the latter is the whole point of hashing it server-side rather than only
  414:    * comparing cookie===header). */
  415:   private assertCsrf(requestContext: { csrfCookieValue: string | undefined; csrfHeaderValue: string | undefined }, session: GuestSession): void {
  416:     if (!requestContext.csrfCookieValue || !requestContext.csrfHeaderValue || requestContext.csrfCookieValue !== requestContext.csrfHeaderValue) {
  417:       throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  418:     }
  419:     if (!guestSessionCsrfMatches(this.pepper, requestContext.csrfHeaderValue, session.csrfTokenHash)) {
  420:       throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  421:     }
  422:   }
  423: 
  424:   /** Layer 3: idempotent evidence submission. `csrfCookieValue`/`csrfHeaderValue` are the raw
  425:    * double-submit pair — mismatch (missing either side, or the two differing) is a CSRF failure
  426:    * and collapses into the same generic error as every other guest-auth failure mode.
  427:    *
  428:    * ADR-0013 (D-265) — now persists a real `DocumentFile` (PENDING_UPLOAD) in the same
  429:    * transaction and returns a presigned PUT, reusing the exact `DocumentFile`/scan pipeline
  430:    * already `APPROVED`/in production for `document`/`subject`/`document-archive` (D-163). Never
  431:    * activates the STARTER/PROMOTER gate (D-193) — inherited unchanged, a guest file follows the
  432:    * identical CLEAN-eligibility path an authenticated upload already does today. */
  433:   async submitEvidence(
  434:     rawSessionToken: string,
  435:     requestContext: { ip: string; csrfCookieValue: string | undefined; csrfHeaderValue: string | undefined },
  436:     input: SubmitEvidenceInput,
  437:   ): Promise<SubmitEvidenceResult> {
  438:     const resolved = await this.resolveSession(rawSessionToken, requestContext);
  439:     this.assertCsrf(requestContext, resolved.session);
  440: 
  441:     const tenantId = authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(resolved.session);
  442:     const subjectId = resolved.session.subjectId;
  443: 
  444:     const idempotencyKey = { PK: `TENANT#${tenantId}#SUBJECT#${subjectId}`, SK: `DOCREQUEST#${resolved.request.documentRequestId}#SUBMIT#${input.idempotencyKey}` };
  445:     // D-243 replay policy (payload-agnostic, first-write-wins, preserves D-143 Decision 4/D-184
  430:    * already `APPROVED`/in production for `document`/`subject`/`document-archive` (D-163). Never
  431:    * activates the STARTER/PROMOTER gate (D-193) — inherited unchanged, a guest file follows the
  432:    * identical CLEAN-eligibility path an authenticated upload already does today. */
  433:   async submitEvidence(
  434:     rawSessionToken: string,
  435:     requestContext: { ip: string; csrfCookieValue: string | undefined; csrfHeaderValue: string | undefined },
  436:     input: SubmitEvidenceInput,
  437:   ): Promise<SubmitEvidenceResult> {
  438:     const resolved = await this.resolveSession(rawSessionToken, requestContext);
  439:     this.assertCsrf(requestContext, resolved.session);
  440: 
  441:     const tenantId = authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(resolved.session);
  442:     const subjectId = resolved.session.subjectId;
  443: 
  444:     const idempotencyKey = { PK: `TENANT#${tenantId}#SUBJECT#${subjectId}`, SK: `DOCREQUEST#${resolved.request.documentRequestId}#SUBMIT#${input.idempotencyKey}` };
  445:     // D-243 replay policy (payload-agnostic, first-write-wins, preserves D-143 Decision 4/D-184
  446:     // unchanged): this short-circuit runs BEFORE `documentTypeId` is ever read/validated below —
  447:     // a replay with the same key and a DIFFERENT `documentTypeId` still returns the original
  448:     // snapshot untouched, never re-validating or comparing the two values. ADR-0013 (Rodada 3):
  449:     // a replay also re-derives the upload offer fresh (never caches a stale `uploadUrl`) — the
  450:     // presign window is bounded by the FILE's own GSI8 deadline, never reopened.
  451:     const existingReplay = await this.store.get<{ resultSnapshot: SubmitEvidenceResult } & EntityKey>(idempotencyKey);
  452:     if (existingReplay) return this.withFreshUploadOffer(tenantId, existingReplay.resultSnapshot);
  453: 
  454:     // ADR-0013 (D-265) — validated in this fixed order (mediaType -> contentLength ->
  455:     // checksumSha256), any failure collapses to the generic guest error, never reaching the
  456:     // transaction below. Schema-level constraints (`docarchive-guest-submit-evidence-request.v1.json`)
  457:     // already enforce the same allowlist/range/pattern before this is reached — this is
  458:     // defense-in-depth at the service boundary, same "double-track" discipline the schema's own
  459:     // comment documents.
  460:     if (!ALLOWED_MEDIA_TYPES.has(input.mediaType)) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  461:     // Codex review round 1 (D-265 implementation): `Number.isInteger` closes a defense-in-depth
  462:     // gap the schema alone already covers (fractional/NaN `contentLength`) but this service-level
  463:     // check didn't — the schema is the HTTP boundary, this is the belt-and-suspenders re-check.
  464:     if (!Number.isInteger(input.contentLength) || input.contentLength <= 0 || input.contentLength > MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  465:     if (!/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/i.test(input.checksumSha256)) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  466: 
  467:     const now = this.now();
  468:     const documentId = this.ids.newDocumentId();
  469:     const versionId = this.ids.newVersionId();
  470:     const fileId = this.ids.newFileId();
  471:     // D-243 (supersedes D-184's conditional guard): `documentTypeId` is now mandatory end to end —
  472:     // the HTTP schema already rejects a missing/renamed field with 400 before this is reached, and
  473:     // the old free-text `documentType ?? requirementId` fallback is removed entirely (no coexistence,
  474:     // clean cutover). Always validated against the tenant's DocumentType catalog via the
  475:     // unconditional ConditionCheck below.
  476:     const documentTypeId = input.documentTypeId;
  477: 
  478:     const document: Document = {
  479:       ...documentKey(tenantId, documentId),
  480:       entityType: "Document",
  690:   }
  691: 
  692:   /**
  693:    * ADR-0013 (D-265) — called by the guest client immediately after S3 confirms 200 on the PUT.
  694:    * Extends the file's GSI8 reconciliation deadline exactly once (`deadlineExtended`), narrowing
  695:    * the race against the reconciliation worker's TIMEOUT sweep down to the network round-trip
  696:    * between the S3 response and this call reaching DynamoDB — never a formal proof of zero race
  697:    * under any adversarial network condition, the same class of guarantee the rest of the scan
  698:    * pipeline already operates under (eventual consistency across S3/GuardDuty/EventBridge/SQS).
  699:    * NEVER touches `scanStatus` or the scan pipeline itself — purely a deadline extension. Resolves
  700:    * `fileId`/`documentId` SOLELY from the idempotency record of the original `submitEvidence()`
  701:    * call (never a raw client-supplied `fileId` — that would not prove possession of the
  702:    * submission).
  703:    */
  704:   async confirmUploadInFlight(
  705:     rawSessionToken: string,
  706:     requestContext: { ip: string; csrfCookieValue: string | undefined; csrfHeaderValue: string | undefined },
  707:     idempotencyKey: string,
  708:   ): Promise<{ extended: boolean }> {
  709:     const resolved = await this.resolveSession(rawSessionToken, requestContext);
  710:     this.assertCsrf(requestContext, resolved.session);
  711: 
  712:     const tenantId = authorizedTenantIdFromPersistedEntity(resolved.session);
  713:     const subjectId = resolved.session.subjectId;
  714:     const idempotencyRecordKey = { PK: `TENANT#${tenantId}#SUBJECT#${subjectId}`, SK: `DOCREQUEST#${resolved.request.documentRequestId}#SUBMIT#${idempotencyKey}` };
  715:     const record = await this.store.get<{ resultSnapshot: SubmitEvidenceResult } & EntityKey>(idempotencyRecordKey);
  716:     if (!record) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError(); // No matching submission for this session — never accepts a loose fileId.
  717: 
  718:     const key = documentFileKey(tenantId, record.resultSnapshot.documentId, record.resultSnapshot.seq, record.resultSnapshot.fileId);
  719: 
  720:     for (let attempt = 0; attempt < MAX_OCC_RETRIES; attempt++) {
  721:       const file = await this.store.get<DocumentFile>(key);
  722:       if (!file || !isNonTerminalFileScanStatus(file.scanStatus)) return { extended: false };
  723:       if (file.deadlineExtended === true) return { extended: true };
  724: 
  725:       // Codex review round 2 (D-265 implementation) — real bug found and fixed: a file can still
  726:       // be `PENDING_UPLOAD` (never advanced to `SCANNING`, meaning no physical S3 event has EVER
  727:       // been observed) while its ORIGINAL GSI8 deadline has already passed — e.g. the guest never
  728:       // actually completed the PUT and retried much later. Extending in that case would falsely
  729:       // resurrect a submission that never received a single byte, and the frontend's own
  730:       // `{extended:true}` == success contract would then lie. `SCANNING` is deliberately EXEMPT
  731:       // from this check — reaching it at all is itself proof a physical event already arrived, so
  732:       // the original deadline having lapsed since then is irrelevant (same reasoning ADR-0013 §3
  733:       // gives for accepting SCANNING unconditionally).
  734:       if (file.scanStatus === "PENDING_UPLOAD") {
  735:         const originalDueAtIso = file.GSI8SK?.split("#TENANT#")[0];
  736:         if (!originalDueAtIso || Date.parse(originalDueAtIso) <= Date.parse(this.now())) return { extended: false };
  737:       }
  738: 
  739:       const due = deriveDocumentFileMaintenanceDue({ scanStatus: file.scanStatus, createdAt: this.now() })!;
  740:       try {
  741:         await this.store.transactWrite([
  742:           {
  743:             Update: buildVersionedUpdate({
  744:               tableName: this.tableName,
  745:               key,
  746:               tenantId,
  747:               expectedVersion: file.version,
  748:               set: { deadlineExtended: true, ...documentFileGsi8Keys({ dueAtIso: due.dueAtIso, tenantId, fileId: file.fileId }) },
  749:               now: this.now(),
  750:               extraConditions: [
  751:                 { expression: "attribute_not_exists(#extended) OR #extended <> :true", names: { "#extended": "deadlineExtended" }, values: { ":true": true } },
  752:                 { expression: "#status IN (:pending, :scanning)", names: { "#status": "scanStatus" }, values: { ":pending": "PENDING_UPLOAD", ":scanning": "SCANNING" } },
  753:               ],
  754:             }),
  755:           },
  115: function requireToken(req: GuestArchiveHttpRequest): string {
  116:   const token = req.pathParameters?.["token"];
  117:   if (!token) throw new ValidationError("Missing token path parameter.");
  118:   return token;
  119: }
  120: 
  121: function cookiesOf(req: GuestArchiveHttpRequest): Record<string, string> {
  122:   return parseCookieHeader(req.headers?.["cookie"] ?? req.headers?.["Cookie"]);
  123: }

  125: /** GET /document-archive/guest/document-requests/{token} — resolves the credential (layer 1)
  126:  * only. Never mints a session (Decision 4). */
  127: export async function handleGetGuestRequest(deps: GuestArchiveHttpDeps, req: GuestArchiveHttpRequest): Promise<GuestArchiveHttpResponse> {
  128:   return withErrorMapping(async () => {
  129:     const token = requireToken(req);
  130:     const resolved = await deps.guestAccess.resolveCredential(token, { ip: req.sourceIp });
  131:     return {
  132:       statusCode: 200,
  133:       headers: baseHeaders(),
  134:       body: {
  135:         request: {
  136:           requirementId: resolved.request.requirementId,
  137:           status: resolved.request.status,
  138:           deadline: resolved.request.deadline,
  139:         },
  140:         // G02 (Block 6, D-2xx) — see ResolvedCredential's own doc comment (guest-document-
  141:         // access-service.ts): best-effort display names, absent when the Subject/Requirement no
  142:         // longer exists.
  143:         subjectDisplayName: resolved.subjectDisplayName,
  144:         requirementName: resolved.requirementName,
  145:       },
  146:     };
  147:   });
  148: }

  150: /** POST /document-archive/guest/document-requests/{token}/session — the explicit human
  151:  * interstitial action that mints a GuestSession (layer 2). Sets the HttpOnly session cookie and
  152:  * the readable CSRF cookie. */
  153: export async function handleStartGuestSession(deps: GuestArchiveHttpDeps, req: GuestArchiveHttpRequest): Promise<GuestArchiveHttpResponse> {
  154:   return withErrorMapping(async () => {
  155:     const token = requireToken(req);
  156:     const result = await deps.guestAccess.startGuestSession(token, { ip: req.sourceIp });
  157:     return {
  158:       statusCode: 201,
  159:       headers: baseHeaders(),
  160:       cookies: [
  161:         buildSetCookieHeader(GUEST_SESSION_COOKIE_NAME, result.session.token, { httpOnly: true, maxAgeSeconds: 30 * 60 }),
  162:         buildSetCookieHeader(GUEST_CSRF_COOKIE_NAME, result.session.csrfToken, { httpOnly: false, maxAgeSeconds: 30 * 60 }),
  163:       ],
  164:       body: { expiresAt: result.expiresAt, subjectDisplayName: result.subjectDisplayName, requirementName: result.requirementName },
  165:     };
  166:   });
  167: }

  169: /** POST /document-archive/guest/document-requests/{token}/uploads — layer 3, idempotent
  170:  * evidence submission. Reads the session token from the HttpOnly cookie (never from the request
  171:  * body/path — the guest never re-types it), CSRF from the cookie+header double-submit pair. */
  172: /** GET /document-archive/guest/document-requests/{token}/document-types — discovery route,

  174:  * the full contradiction/pendency writeup). Same anti-enumeration posture as every other guest
  175:  * handler: token validation failures collapse into the identical `GuestAccessInvalidError`

  177: export async function handleListGuestDocumentTypes(deps: GuestArchiveHttpDeps, req: GuestArchiveHttpRequest): Promise<GuestArchiveHttpResponse> {
  178:   return withErrorMapping(async () => {
  179:     const token = requireToken(req);
  180:     const documentTypes = await deps.guestAccess.listActiveDocumentTypesForGuest(token, { ip: req.sourceIp });
  181:     return {
  182:       statusCode: 200,
  183:       headers: baseHeaders(),
  184:       body: { documentTypes },
  185:     };
  186:   });
  187: }

  189: export async function handleSubmitEvidence(deps: GuestArchiveHttpDeps, req: GuestArchiveHttpRequest<SubmitEvidenceInput>): Promise<GuestArchiveHttpResponse> {
  190:   return withErrorMapping(async () => {
  191:     requireToken(req); // Presence-checked for route symmetry/observability; resolution is by session cookie, not the path token.
  192:     // Pre-existing gap, found (not introduced) by Codex review round 1 of D-264 (Block 6, G02
  193:     // frontend): the session cookie (GUEST_SESSION_COOKIE_NAME) is a single global cookie per
  194:     // browser, never bound to/verified against THIS route's own `token` path segment. Two guest
  195:     // links open in two tabs of the same browser share one cookie jar - the second `session`
  196:     // mint overwrites the first tab's cookie, so the first tab's later `uploads` call resolves
  197:     // against the SECOND session/DocumentRequest, not its own. Real since D-146 (a single-link-
  198:     // at-a-time design); only now reachable via a real multi-tab UI. Fixing this means binding
  199:     // the session to its issuing token (or scoping the cookie name by token) - a session-identity
  200:     // design change that deserves its own scoping, not a rushed patch. Named in decisions-log
  201:     // D-264.
  202:     // ADR-0013 (D-265) implementation, Codex review round 1: a missing body used to throw a
  203:     // differentiated `ValidationError` (400 with detail) while an invalid one collapsed to the
  204:     // generic guest error — an anti-enumeration leak the schema-validation collapse below was
  205:     // meant to close but didn't cover. Both now collapse identically.
  206:     if (!req.body) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  207:     validateGuestSubmitSchema(req.body);
  208: 
  209:     const cookies = cookiesOf(req);
  210:     const sessionToken = cookies[GUEST_SESSION_COOKIE_NAME];
  211:     // A missing session cookie is itself a guest-auth failure mode (no session to resolve) —
  212:     // collapses into the same generic error as a malformed/expired/wrong-secret token, never a
  213:     // differentiated 400 that would tell a caller "you're just missing a cookie" (an oracle).
  214:     if (!sessionToken) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  215:     const csrfHeaderValue = req.headers?.[CSRF_HEADER_NAME] ?? req.headers?.["X-CSRF-Token"];
  216: 
  217:     const result = await deps.guestAccess.submitEvidence(
  218:       sessionToken,
  219:       { ip: req.sourceIp, csrfCookieValue: cookies[GUEST_CSRF_COOKIE_NAME], csrfHeaderValue },
  220:       req.body,
  221:     );
  222:     return { statusCode: 201, headers: baseHeaders(), body: result as unknown as Record<string, unknown> };
  223:   });
  224: }
  225: 
  226: const CONFIRM_UPLOAD_SCHEMA_ID = "https://expiration-tracker/schemas/api/docarchive-guest-confirm-upload-request.v1.json";
  227: 
  228: /** PATCH /document-archive/guest/document-requests/{token}/uploads — ADR-0013 (D-265). Same
  229:  * path as `handleSubmitEvidence` (a different HTTP verb, not a new CloudFront behavior — the
  230:  * existing guest-uploads CloudFront cache behavior already matches this exact path regardless
  231:  * of method), same session-cookie/CSRF discipline. */
  232: export async function handleConfirmUpload(deps: GuestArchiveHttpDeps, req: GuestArchiveHttpRequest<{ idempotencyKey: string }>): Promise<GuestArchiveHttpResponse> {
  233:   return withErrorMapping(async () => {
  234:     requireToken(req);
  235:     // Same anti-enumeration collapse as handleSubmitEvidence above — missing and invalid bodies
  236:     // must be indistinguishable to the caller.
  237:     if (!req.body) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  238:     try {
  239:       validateAgainstSchema(CONFIRM_UPLOAD_SCHEMA_ID, req.body);
  240:     } catch {
  241:       throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  242:     }
  243: 
  244:     const cookies = cookiesOf(req);
  245:     const sessionToken = cookies[GUEST_SESSION_COOKIE_NAME];
  246:     if (!sessionToken) throw new GuestAccessInvalidError();
  247:     const csrfHeaderValue = req.headers?.[CSRF_HEADER_NAME] ?? req.headers?.["X-CSRF-Token"];
  248: 
  249:     const result = await deps.guestAccess.confirmUploadInFlight(
  250:       sessionToken,
  251:       { ip: req.sourceIp, csrfCookieValue: cookies[GUEST_CSRF_COOKIE_NAME], csrfHeaderValue },
  252:       req.body.idempotencyKey,
  253:     );
  254:     return { statusCode: 200, headers: baseHeaders(), body: result as unknown as Record<string, unknown> };
  255:   });
  256: }

  265: 
    1: /**
    2:  * RequestAccessCredential — D-143 Decision 4 (guest access, layer 1 of 3). Long-duration,
    3:  * revocable `selector.secret` pair granting an external (non-authenticated) party the right to
    4:  * view ONE `DocumentRequest` and, via `GuestSession`, eventually upload evidence for it. TTL
    5:  * equals the business Request's deadline (Decision 4: "TTL = prazo do Request de negócio") —
    6:  * never a fixed literal like the older subject-module `GuestTokenPointer`'s 14 days.
    7:  *
    8:  * Crypto (deliberate deviation from the design doc's illustrative "Argon2id" wording, traceable
    9:  * per D-143 Decision 4's E-014 research declaration — SIM, NIST SP 800-63B-4 §5.1.2): this
   10:  * codebase has ZERO Argon2id anywhere (verified by grep before writing this file). Argon2id's
   11:  * memory-hardness exists to slow an OFFLINE brute-force search against a LOW-entropy secret
   12:  * (a human-chosen password drawn from a small effective keyspace). The `secret` half of this
   13:  * credential is 256 bits of `randomBytes` — a high-entropy secret NIST SP 800-63B-4 §5.1.2
   14:  * classifies as not needing a memory-hard KDF at all; a plain keyed hash already makes offline
   15:  * guessing infeasible (2^256 search space), and Argon2id would only add real CPU/memory cost
   16:  * to every legitimate verification for no corresponding security gain. This module therefore
   17:  * reuses the exact HMAC-SHA256 + versioned pepper + `timingSafeEqual` + dummy-hash anti-timing
   18:  * pattern already established and battle-tested by `src/modules/subject/domain/guest-token.ts`
   19:  * (same architecture, same reasoning applies identically to this credential's secret shape) —
   20:  * not a new `argon2` dependency.
   21:  *
   22:  * Lookup via tenantless pointer item (`DOCARCHIVEGUEST#<selectorHash>`/`POINTER`) — another
   23:  * instance of the same documented tenantless-partition exception `guest-token.ts` names as its
   24:  * own "third exception" (after `IdentityMapping` and GSI3): the lookup happens BEFORE `tenantId`
   25:  * is known, so the PK cannot begin with `TENANT#<tenantId>`.
   26:  */
   27: import { createHmac, randomBytes, timingSafeEqual } from "node:crypto";
   28: import type { EntityKey } from "../../../shared/dynamodb/occ.js";
   29: 
   30: export interface RequestAccessCredential extends EntityKey {
   31:   SK: "POINTER";
   32:   entityType: "RequestAccessCredential";
   33:   selectorHash: string;
   34:   secretHash: string;
   35:   tenantId: string;
   36:   subjectId: string;
   37:   requirementId: string;
   38:   documentRequestId: string;
   39:   tokenVersion: number;
   40:   expiresAt: string;
   41:   /** DynamoDB TTL attribute (epoch seconds) — same D-047/D-048 lesson `GuestTokenPointer`
   42:    * documents: `expiresAt` alone is only ever read by application code, never triggers physical
   43:    * deletion on its own. */
   44:   purgeAfterTtl: number;
   45:   revokedAt?: string;
   46:   createdAt: string;
   47:   updatedAt: string;
   48:   version: number;
   49: }
   50: 
   51: export function requestAccessCredentialKey(selectorHash: string): { PK: string; SK: "POINTER" } {
   52:   return { PK: `DOCARCHIVEGUEST#${selectorHash}`, SK: "POINTER" };
   53: }
   54: 
   55: export function epochSecondsFromIso(iso: string): number {
   56:   return Math.floor(Date.parse(iso) / 1000);
   57: }
   58: 
   59: /** Pepper is injected from config/Secrets Manager by the composition root — this module only
   60:  * implements the hash/compare mechanics, same separation as `guest-token.ts`'s `GuestTokenCrypto`. */
   61: export interface RequestAccessCrypto {
   62:   hash(pepper: string, value: string): string;
   63: }
   64: 
   65: export const hmacRequestAccessCrypto: RequestAccessCrypto = {
   66:   hash(pepper: string, value: string): string {
   67:     return createHmac("sha256", pepper).update(value).digest("hex");
   68:   },
   69: };
   70: 
   71: export interface IssuedRequestAccessCredential {
   72:   /** Full `selector.secret` value embedded in the link sent to the external party — never
   73:    * persisted raw. */
   74:   token: string;
   75:   selector: string;
   76:   selectorHash: string;
   77:   secretHash: string;
   78: }
   79: 
   80: /** Generates a new high-entropy selector/secret pair (128/256 bits respectively — same sizing
   81:  * as `guest-token.ts`'s `issueGuestToken`). */
   82: export function issueRequestAccessCredential(pepper: string, crypto: RequestAccessCrypto = hmacRequestAccessCrypto): IssuedRequestAccessCredential {
   83:   const selector = randomBytes(16).toString("hex");
   84:   const secret = randomBytes(32).toString("hex");
   85:   return {
   86:     token: `${selector}.${secret}`,
   87:     selector,
   88:     selectorHash: crypto.hash(pepper, selector),
   89:     secretHash: crypto.hash(pepper, secret),
   90:   };
   91: }
   92: 
   93: export interface ParsedRequestAccessToken {
   94:   selector: string;
   95:   secret: string;
   96: }
   97: 
   98: /** Parse never throws — malformed input takes the same dummy-comparison path as every other
   99:  * failure mode (anti-enumeration). */
  100: export function parseRequestAccessToken(raw: string): ParsedRequestAccessToken | undefined {
  101:   const parts = raw.split(".");
  102:   if (parts.length !== 2) return undefined;
  103:   const [selector, secret] = parts;
  104:   if (!selector || !secret || !/^[a-f0-9]{32}$/.test(selector) || !/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/.test(secret)) return undefined;
  105:   return { selector, secret };
  106: }
  107: 
  108: /** `timingSafeEqual` comparison — never `===` on a value derived from a secret. */
  109: export function requestAccessSecretMatches(pepper: string, secret: string, expectedSecretHash: string, crypto: RequestAccessCrypto = hmacRequestAccessCrypto): boolean {
  110:   const actual = Buffer.from(crypto.hash(pepper, secret), "hex");
  111:   const expected = Buffer.from(expectedSecretHash, "hex");
  112:   if (actual.length !== expected.length) return false;
  113:   return timingSafeEqual(actual, expected);
  114: }
  115:

codex
O núcleo confere: a sessão já persiste o hash do selector emissor; as duas mutações resolvem exclusivamente o cookie; expiração da sessão e liveness/cancelamento do `DocumentRequest` são rechecados antes de qualquer efeito. Estou fechando agora a análise de call sites e de um possível detalhe semântico: ignorar deliberadamente o `secret` do token do path.

docs/architecture\reviews\m3.5-runtime-design\codex-output-implementation-revalidation.txt:93:Judgment calls (blueprint silente): (1) evento `ItemDueDateChanged` segue exatamente o schema já existente desde M0 (`schemas/events/item-due-date-changed.v1.json`: `itemId`/`previousDueDate`/`newDueDate`/`itemVersion`, `additionalProperties: false`) em vez do exemplo mais rico do texto do blueprint §8.3 (que inclui `timeZone`/`reminderPolicyId`/`changeReason`) — o schema já testado/versionado é a fonte de verdade de contrato per `AGENTS.md` §6, o exemplo em prosa não; campos adicionais ficam para quando M3 (Reminder) precisar deles, exigindo `.v2` aditivo; (2) `renewItem` também dispara `ItemDueDateChanged` (para o item novo, `previousDueDate: null`) — o blueprint só descreve esse evento no contexto de `updateItem`, mas renovação também é uma "mudança de vencimento" do ponto de vista do futuro `ReminderProducer` (M3), e a alternativa (renovação silenciosa) deixaria o dashboard/scheduler sem sinal de que um novo item precisa de agendamento; (3) `archiveItem` usa a ação de autorização `item:update` (não uma ação dedicada — a matriz de M1 não define uma para archive) enquanto `deleteItem` usa `item:delete` (ADMIN_ROLES), seguindo a granularidade que já existe; (4) idempotência de renovação aceita uma `idempotencyKey` explícita do chamador (header `Idempotency-Key`) com fallback determinístico `itemId|expectedVersion|cycle` quando ausente, já que o blueprint define a CHAVE de dedupe mas não de onde ela vem por HTTP; (5) `ExpirationStore.transactWrite` foi adicionado ao port (não existe em `IdentityStore`) porque nenhuma escrita de M1 precisava de `TransactWriteItems` multi-item — a fake em memória (`test/unit/expiration/in-memory-store.ts`) só entende as duas formas exatas de `ConditionExpression` que este código produz (mesma limitação documentada, mesmo espírito de `InMemoryIdentityStore`).

docs/architecture\blocker-b-recon-handoff.md:54:- `docs/engineering/principles.md` princípio #4, diretamente aplicável ao redesign: "idempotência/outbox/reconciliação existem para que uma falha de fila/rede nunca perca um lembrete silenciosamente — mas o inverso também vale: a materialização de ocorrências não pode ficar bloqueada esperando o outbox confirmar publicação." Ou seja, o novo trigger de materialização (seja síncrono no create/update/renew, seja via evento) não deve tornar a escrita do item/policy dependente da confirmação de um efeito colateral assíncrono.
docs/architecture\blocker-b-recon-handoff.md:80:3. Se `createItem`/`updateItem`/`renewItem` (em `src/modules/expiration/**`) chamam o materializer sincronamente, de alguma forma, ou não chamam de forma alguma.
docs/architecture\blocker-b-recon-handoff.md:95:**Hipótese de trabalho (confirmada por 3 fontes documentais independentes, ainda não confirmada em código — fork de recon rodando):** o `ReminderMaterializer` (ou equivalente) só é chamado pelo caminho de reconciliação de DST (`reminder-reconciliation`, GSI6 `WORKSTATE#DST_PENDING`); nenhuma rota HTTP de `ReminderPolicy` nem `createItem`/`updateItem`/`renewItem` de `ExpirationItem` chama o materializer ou emite evento que o dispare. Isso explicaria exatamente o sintoma relatado: salvar uma policy hoje não produz nenhuma ocorrência futura, exceto no raro caso em que uma ocorrência pré-existente cruza uma transição de DST.
docs/architecture\blocker-b-recon-handoff.md:103:- Onde plugar o trigger: síncrono dentro da mesma transação de `createItem`/`updateItem`/`renewItem`/policy-save (mais simples, mas acopla a escrita do item à materialização — tensão com o princípio #4 de engenharia citado acima) vs. assíncrono via outbox/evento (`ReminderPolicySaved`/`ExpirationItemCreated` → materializer como consumidor, consistente com o padrão outbox já usado em M3/M3.5) vs. híbrido.
docs/architecture\blocker-b-recon-handoff.md:105:- Renovação: confirmar que ocorrências do ciclo antigo não ficam entregáveis após `renewItem` — a materialização precisa saber invalidar/versionar ocorrências futuras do ciclo anterior, não só criar novas.
docs/architecture\data-model.md:31:| ExpirationItem | itemId, nome, categoria, descrição, dueDate, issueDate, periodicidade, emissor, número, responsável, tags, prioridade, status (ACTIVE/ARCHIVED/RENEWED/DELETED), renewedFromId?, version | `TENANT#t#ITEM#i` / `META` |
docs/architecture\decisions-log.md:74:| D-054 | Amendment ao D-053 (Full BFF), a partir de auditoria adversarial independente trazida por Marcelo com 16 pontos técnicos específicos — protocolo Claude↔Codex completo (Rodada A: Claude verifica os 16 pontos contra código real + fontes externas atuais, RFC 9700/RFC 10017 confirmados; Rodada B: Codex achou 4 furos reais nas correções da Rodada A; Rodada C: furos corrigidos, inclusive verificação externa dedicada de uma alegação factual do Codex antes de aceitá-la; Rodada D: Codex confirma). **Não reabre Full BFF** — endurece 6 pontos antes da implementação: (1) mecanismo de refresh reescrito — rotação nativa do Cognito (`RefreshTokenRotation`, grace period ~30s) como fonte de verdade de "replay real" em vez de contador de geração local (que criava falso-positivo sob concorrência legítima), com lease BFF-side (fencing token, adquirida antes da chamada ao Cognito) só como otimização de latência; exige remover `ALLOW_REFRESH_TOKEN_AUTH` do app client Cognito (mutuamente exclusivo com rotation, confirmado via documentação AWS) — vestigial para o Full BFF, que só usa OAuth2/Hosted UI; (2) sessão migrada de um ponteiro na tabela single-table para uma **tabela DynamoDB nova, dedicada, IAM-isolada só para o BFF** — achado real: a política `tenant_facing_read_write` já concede `GetItem`/`Query` na tabela base a ~20+ roles de Lambda de recurso, então cifrar só o refresh token (D-053 Rodada 4) não protegia o `codeVerifier`/metadados de sessão, todos legíveis por qualquer handler existente; mesmo princípio de isolamento explícito já usado para GSI3/GSI6, aplicado a uma tabela; (3) dois cookies com `SameSite` diferenciado — login (`Lax`, sobrevive ao redirect cross-site do Hosted UI) e sessão (`Strict`, só nasce após o callback validado via redirect same-origin); (4) lifetime de sessão explícito — `absoluteExpiresAt` fixo na criação (nunca estendido por refresh) + idle timeout via `purgeAfterTtl`, ausente de todo documento normativo antes desta auditoria; (5) `RevokeToken` best-effort no logout por dispositivo, sempre após a revogação local (que continua fonte de verdade); docs/architecture\decisions-log.md:98:| D-078 | W3-07 — mesma sessão de D-076/D-077, item 3 do prompt de retomada: mitigação defensiva barata para o risco de admissão parcial em `import-service.ts`'s `reserveImport()`, sem forçar a decisão de produto (unificar quota+idempotency+job-create numa única transação maior, trade-off latência-vs-atomicidade) que D-074/D-075 deixaram deliberadamente para o dono do produto. `idempotency.begin()` e as duas reservas `quota.consume()` (IMPORT_COUNT/IMPORT_BYTES) continuam fora da transação fenceada que cria o `ImportJob` — isso NÃO muda. O que muda: quando a criação fenceada falha (rejeição da fence OU um conflito OCC comum), o `catch` agora libera best-effort o que já tinha sido reservado — `idempotency.abort()` (a mesma primitiva já usada por `renewItem`'s caminho de conflito OCC, "idempotency liveness residual") e `quota.release()` para os dois tipos — antes de relançar o erro original. Toda compensação roda via `Promise.allSettled` (nunca deixa uma falha de compensação mascarar o erro real nem travar a resposta ao chamador). Sem isso: uma rejeição deixava a chave de idempotência presa em `IN_PROGRESS` para sempre (todo retry, mesmo depois do tenant voltar a ACTIVE, batia em `ConcurrentOperationError` permanentemente) e a quota consumida vazava até expirar a janela — com isso, uma rejeição volta o tenant ao estado pré-tentativa (quota liberada, idempotency key livre para nova tentativa), sem alterar o que já era aceito por design para essa classe de risco (uma falha DEPOIS da transação fenceada, ex. entre commit e presign, continua não coberta — fora do escopo desta mitigação). **Achado real de infraestrutura de teste descoberto e corrigido nesta sessão**: `InMemoryImportStore.transactWrite` (o fake usado pelos testes de `ImportService`) só reconhecia a convenção `#version = :expectedVersion`/`#setN`/`:setN` de `buildVersionedUpdate` para condição e aplicação de `Update` — a condição `#status = :expected` de `transitionIdempotencyStatus` (usada por `idempotency.abort()`) nunca era avaliada (sempre "passava" silenciosamente) e o SET correspondente (`#status`/`#requestHash`/`#responseRef`/`#completedAt`, nomes literais, não a convenção `#setN`) nunca era aplicado — ou seja, `abort()` "funcionava" sem erro mas não mudava nada de verdade, mascarando completamente a mitigação nos testes. Generalizado para um parser genérico de `ConditionExpression`/`UpdateExpression` (`splitTopLevelAnd`/`evaluateEqualityClause`/`applyUpdateExpression`) que avalia QUALQUER cláusula de igualdade `#name = :value` e aplica QUALQUER `SET`/`REMOVE` nomeado, não só a convenção `#setN` — acerta tanto o caso antigo quanto o novo sem duplicar lógica. **IMPLEMENTED + UNIT TESTED** (2 testes adversariais novos em `test/unit/import/import-service.test.ts`: rejeição por DELETING libera a quota IMPORT_COUNT/IMPORT_BYTES de volta a 0; rejeição por DELETING aborta o idempotency record, permitindo retry bem-sucedido com a MESMA Idempotency-Key uma vez o tenant voltando a ACTIVE). 1034 testes de backend passando (era 1032), typecheck/lint/check-boundaries/check-docs limpos, zero regressão | Type 1 (nível 3-4, mitigação defensiva mecânica que não altera a fronteira transacional nem o trade-off já identificado como decisão de produto) | N/A — mitigação de menor esforço já sugerida pelo Codex em D-075, não uma nova decisão de arquitetura | N/A | **IMPLEMENTED + UNIT TESTED** — a admissão parcial em si (quota/idempotency fora da transação fenceada) permanece decisão de produto pendente, mas o vazamento residual de uma tentativa REJEITADA (quota presa, idempotency key presa) está fechado | `src/modules/import/application/import-service.ts`, `test/unit/import/import-service.test.ts`, `test/unit/import/in-memory-store.ts` | 2026-08-29 |

docs/architecture\decisions-log.md:161:| D-141 | D-136/D-B implementado: session touch coalescing. `resolveSession()`'s idle-TTL bump em `bff-auth-service.ts` deixa de escrever a cada resolução e passa a só escrever quando o tempo restante antes do idle timeout cair abaixo de `SESSION_IDLE_RENEWAL_THRESHOLD_SECONDS` (1 dia, nova constante em `cookies.ts`), dentro da janela de 7 dias (`SESSION_IDLE_TTL_SECONDS`). `ConsistentRead` na leitura inicial permanece intocado (D-136 exigiu isso explicitamente — correção de revogação importa mais que custo de leitura). **Achado real confirmado durante a implementação**: a condição anterior (`nextPurge !== session.purgeAfterTtl`) parecia um coalescing mas não era um na prática — `nextPurge` deriva de `epochSeconds(now)`, que difere do `now` do bump anterior em quase toda request real, então a escrita disparava quase sempre mesmo antes desta mudança (bate com o achado da auditoria original: "1 session write por request" já era o comportamento real de produção). **G-V3 real, não trivial**: a primeira tentativa de prova (comparar `.version`/`.purgeAfterTtl` do valor RETORNADO por `resolveSession()`) sempre passava independente da mutação, porque `resolveSession()` nunca reatribui seu snapshot local de `session` após uma escrita de bump bem-sucedida — o valor retornado sempre reflete a leitura pré-bump, um detalhe pré-existente não relacionado a esta mudança. docs/architecture\decisions-log.md:237:| D-206 | **Roadmap P1 item 17 ("Bulk actions") — DESIGN, protocolo Claude↔Codex completo, 4 rodadas** (`docs/architecture/reviews/bulk-actions-scoping/`). Fecha o próximo item do backlog P1 depois de D-205 (item 16). Bulk archive foi anteriormente classificado como "product creep" por falta de base em roadmap (`docs/frontend/interface-context-and-critical-tasks.md` §30) — condição que não vale mais, roadmap agora nomeia o item formalmente. **Declaração E-014: SIM PARCIAL** (Atlassian Jira bulk operations — job assíncrono/`taskId`/polling até 1000 itens — e Asana multi-select síncrono, fontes primárias 2026-09-05; padrão de mercado é split síncrono-para-pequeno/assíncrono-para-grande, este design fica no lado síncrono por proporcionalidade de escala, teto 100 não 1000). **6 achados reais do Codex, todos verificados por leitura de código, fecharam as 4 rodadas**: (1) bulk archive é mais destrutivo (`ItemDeactivated`+cancelamento de reminders, `expiration-service.ts:795`) — fechado com confirmação UX explícita obrigatória; (2) faltava contrato `{itemId, expectedVersion}` por item — sem ele perderia a garantia OCC — fechado, obrigatório; (3) teto 100 síncrono sobre o timeout real de 10s de `items_handler` (`lambda-function/variables.tf:43`) — fechado com Lambda DEDICADA (mesmo precedente de `export-handler`, D-123/D-126), `timeout_seconds=25`, dentro da cota real de 30s do HTTP API v2 (`api-gateway/main.tf:6`, correção de "REST 29s"); (4) justificativa "export processa 2000 itens" era analogia fraca (leitura vs. transação) — fechado reclassificando o teto como hipótese de engenharia condicionada a smoke test antes de produção; (5) ambiguidade de retry pós-timeout (itens já aplicados voltariam como `VERSION_CONFLICT`) — fechado com regra de reconciliação restrita (`TARGET_ALREADY_APPLIED` só quando version avançou exatamente 1 E o estado bate EXATAMENTE com o alvo, nunca afirmando autoria da tentativa); (6) `archiveItem()` atual não bloqueia rearquivar item já `ARCHIVED` com versão válida — fechado com precondição `status === "ACTIVE"` no bulk archive (`INELIGIBLE_STATE` se não elegível), comportamento single-item existente intocado. **9 decisões finais** (detalhe completo em `estado-final-consolidado.md`): lista v1 restrita a bulk reassign+archive; confirmação UX+precondição ACTIVE-only para archive; contrato por item com `expectedVersion`; fan-out via transações pequenas independentes (padrão de `import-commit-service.ts`, D-192); reconciliação de retry sem ledger; resposta síncrona com taxonomia completa de outcome; Lambda dedicada 25s com teto 100 condicionado a validação empírica; RBAC `WRITE_ROLES` sem elevação artificial. **Fora de escopo, nomeado**: bulk delete/renew/Requirement/Document; job assíncrono/ledger (só se autoria de tentativa virar requisito); teto acima de 100. DESIGN-ONLY — nenhum código/schema/infra alterado. | Nível 5 (novo mecanismo de fan-out transacional + nova Lambda dedicada + nova rota HTTP) | 9,1/10 (Rodada 4, autoavaliação às cegas) | 9,2/10 (Rodada 4, final) | **DESIGN APROVADO — implementação real fica para sessão(ões) dedicada(s) futura(s), mesmo padrão de D-121/D-127/D-179/D-191/D-194/D-197/D-204/D-205.** Fatias sugeridas (não decididas): (1) Lambda dedicada+rotas HTTP+RBAC; (2) fan-out+reconciliação+taxonomia de outcome; (3) smoke test de integração medindo custo real de 100 itens antes de expor em produção. | `docs/architecture/reviews/bulk-actions-scoping/` | 2026-09-05 |

CRUD completo (`createItem`/`getItem`/`updateItem`/`archiveItem`/`deleteItem`) mais `renewItem` (cria item sucessor com `renewedFromId`, nunca muta `dueDate` no agregado de origem — o item de origem transiciona para `RENEWED`), todos via `RequestContextResolver`+`authorize()` de M1 (nenhuma authz reimplementada). OCC via `shared/dynamodb/occ.ts` em toda escrita; mudança de `dueDate` (em `updateItem` e em `renewItem`) dispara `expiration.item-due-date-changed.v1` por `shared/outbox/outbox.ts` na MESMA transação do item; `AuditEvent` append-only (`TENANT#t#AUDIT#<yyyyMM>`/`EVT#<timestamp>#<id>`, diffs redigidos via `Redactor` de M0) gravado em toda mutação, também na mesma transação — nenhum caminho de update/delete exposto para o próprio audit. Dashboard via GSI1 (`queryGsi1`, filtro por `TENANT#t#ITEMSTATUS#<status>`, ordenado por `dueDate`). Renovação é idempotente (`tenantId|sourceItemId|sourceVersion|cycle`, reutilizando `IdempotencyStore` de M0 via adapter) — bug real pego pelo próprio teste de idempotência: a checagem de status `ACTIVE` rodava antes do `idempotency.begin()`, então uma segunda chamada idêntica falhava porque a origem já estava `RENEWED` pela primeira; corrigido movendo a checagem de status para depois do idempotency check. Rotas HTTP CDK adicionadas (`POST /items`, `GET /items/{itemId}`, `PUT /items/{itemId}`, `DELETE /items/{itemId}`, `POST /items/{itemId}/archive`, `POST /items/{itemId}/renew`, `GET /items/dashboard`) atrás do mesmo authorizer JWT, um único `ScopedLambdaFunction` (`ItemsHandler`) por convenção de Lambda-monolito. 21 testes novos (134 total no repo): unit de `ExpirationService` (OCC/outbox/audit/renew/idempotência/isolamento por tenant), suíte de integração `test/integration/expiration-lifecycle.test.ts` (exit criterion do marco: create→read→update com mudança de vencimento→renew→archive→soft-delete via handlers HTTP reais, mais isolamento cross-tenant), contrato para `item-due-date-changed.v1` (schema já existia desde M0 mas sem teste — adicionado agora) e synth CDK das novas rotas. `typecheck`/`lint`/`test`/`validate-schemas` verdes. Implementação direta de decisão já aprovada (`AGENTS.md` §4). `AGENTS.md` §7 (então §6, antes da reestruturação que inseriu a seção de branch strategy) e `NEXT_SESSION_PROMPT.md` atualizados; nada commitado. `ARCHITECTURE STATUS` permanece `NOT APPROVED`.

docs/architecture\w3-07-writer-inventory.md:26:| `ExpirationService.commit()` (backs `createItem`/`updateItem`/`archiveItem`/`deleteItem`/`renewItem`) | `expiration/application/expiration-service.ts` | Yes — shared `transactWrite` (single choke point — every one of the 5 public mutations already funneled through this one private method before this session) | **Fenced via TenantBusinessMutation (THIS SESSION, chunk 9/N)** — `commit()` now takes a `tenantId` parameter and routes its transaction through `executeTenantBusinessMutation`; because every mutation already shared this one method, fencing it here fences all 5 callers at once. `TenantNotActiveError` is rethrown unchanged (never folded into `ConflictError("VERSION_CONFLICT")`), so callers' existing idempotency abort-on-catch logic still runs correctly and a caller can distinguish "tenant is being deleted" from an ordinary OCC conflict on the aggregate itself | N/A (synchronous DynamoDB write) — a mutation admitted while ACTIVE (including one already inside an idempotency-protected `createItem`/`renewItem` call) completes normally even if DELETING starts moments later; only NEW admissions after that point are blocked | Yes — `test/unit/expiration/expiration-service.test.ts` (5 new adversarial tests: ACTIVE control case; DELETING rejects createItem with no row left behind; DELETING rejects updateItem/archiveItem/deleteItem/renewItem all atomically with the pre-DELETING item state unchanged; an ordinary OCC version conflict on updateItem is still `ConflictError`, not misclassified as the fence, proving the `CancellationReasons`-index fix — see `tenant-business-mutation.ts` — applies here too; a retried `createItem` idempotency replay after DELETING starts still returns the cached result rather than being blocked). Real gap found and fixed as a byproduct: `test/unit/expiration/in-memory-store.ts`'s `transactWrite` fake threw fail-fast with no `CancellationReasons` at all — every `TransactionCanceledException` (including an ordinary OCC conflict on the caller's own Update entry, e.g. a stale `expectedVersion`) would have been misclassified as `TenantNotActiveError` once the fence was wired in, exactly the bug `TenantQuotaService.consume()`'s migration found and fixed in the real fake; extended to populate per-entry `CancellationReasons` the same way. Also downstream: `test/unit/reminder/reminder-materialization-trigger.test.ts` (13 tests) and `test/integration/expiration-lifecycle.test.ts` (2 tests) needed `TenantLifecycleRecord` seeding to keep passing — smaller blast radius than the ~600-test worst case originally feared (the shared `commit()` choke point meant seeding once per store fixture, not once per test) |
docs/architecture\w3-07-writer-inventory.md:41:| S3 presigned upload issuance | `document/application/document-service.ts` `reserveUpload()` (600s TTL, confirmed against code), `import/application/import-service.ts` `reserveImport()` (900s TTL = 15*60, confirmed against code), `subject/application/guest-submission-service.ts` `startSubmission()` (600s TTL, confirmed) | **Real DynamoDB writes exist at issuance time on the NEW-reservation path in 2 of the 3 call sites** — `document-service.ts` already had its own `transactWrite` (Document+UploadSlot Put), `import-service.ts` had a bare unfenced `putIfAbsent` (ImportJob Put); `guest-submission-service.ts` covered separately above | **Fenced via TenantBusinessMutation (THIS SESSION, chunk 8/N)**, for `document-service.ts` and `import-service.ts` — deviates from the design doc's original "read-then-check before presign, don't force through a transaction" framing: real code inspection found each of these two already has (or, for import, was trivially convertible to) its own tenant-scoped DynamoDB write immediately before the presign call, so fencing THAT write via `executeTenantBusinessMutation` (same established pattern as every other writer) is strictly more correct than a separate unfenced read-check bolted on right before `presignUpload()` — it closes an actual writer-inventory gap (the Document/UploadSlot/ImportJob creation itself was never fenced) AND blocks new presign issuance as a direct consequence, atomically, with no separate TOCTOU window. Idempotent retries (`COMPLETED_SAME_REQUEST` branch, no new write) are NOT re-fenced and may still re-presign, per the established "admitted while ACTIVE may finish" contract. The **residual TTL-window risk remains accepted and unchanged** for all 3 call sites: a URL issued on the ACQUIRED/new-admission path a moment before the fence would have rejected it is impossible now (same transaction), but a URL already issued before DELETING started remains usable until its TTL regardless — this session does not (and per the design doc, should not) attempt to revoke already-issued capabilities. | A URL issued while ACTIVE remains usable until its TTL even after DELETING starts; the resulting object is quarantined/evidence-only until the (fenced) promotion step admits it | Yes — `test/unit/document/document-service.test.ts` and `test/unit/import/import-service.test.ts`, both with ACTIVE control case + DELETING adversarial test proving the fence blocks the write (and therefore the presign) with no row left behind. `test/unit/import/in-memory-store.ts`'s fake previously silently NO-OPed on `ConditionCheck` entries (a fence added there would have gone completely untested) — extended to evaluate them with the same `CancellationReasons` convention as the other module fakes, same fix `document-service.ts`'s handler test needed for the resolver-bootstrapped tenantId to reach the (separate, in-memory) `DocumentStore`/`ImportStore` fakes. |

NOTA: 9,4/10  
VEREDITO: APPROVED

ACHADOS:

1. MENOR — `src/modules/document-archive/domain/guest-session.ts:34`: a implementação deverá atualizar o comentário que classifica `credentialSelectorHash` como “audit trail only, never used for authorization”. Após o vínculo proposto, essa documentação ficará factualmente incorreta.

2. MENOR — `round1-claude-proposal.md`, seção “Proposta de desenho”: falta explicitar os testes adversariais obrigatórios para ambas as mutações: token A/sessão B rejeitado, mesmo token/sessão aceito, token malformado rejeitado genericamente, sessão expirada rejeitada e `DocumentRequest` cancelado entre emissão e mutação rejeitado. O código atual confirma que expiração e liveness já são rechecadas em `resolveSession()`, mas o novo vínculo precisa de cobertura própria.

3. MENOR — `round1-claude-proposal.md`, item 2: “antes de qualquer efeito colateral” não é literalmente correto, pois `resolveSession()` já consome o rate limiter antes da checagem do vínculo. Isso não compromete a segurança nem o fechamento do bug; apenas significa que uma tentativa com sessão válida e path incompatível consumirá quota.

Nenhum achado bloqueante. Comparar os hashes de selector com `===` é adequado: trata-se de identidade opaca do recurso, não de validação do secret. Ignorar o secret do path preserva corretamente a decisão aprovada de que a sessão é autossuficiente após sua emissão. As três rotas restantes autenticam diretamente o token completo do path e não apresentam a colisão cookie/path.

58.140
NOTA: 9,4/10  
VEREDITO: APPROVED

ACHADOS:

1. MENOR — `src/modules/document-archive/domain/guest-session.ts:34`: a implementação deverá atualizar o comentário que classifica `credentialSelectorHash` como “audit trail only, never used for authorization”. Após o vínculo proposto, essa documentação ficará factualmente incorreta.

2. MENOR — `round1-claude-proposal.md`, seção “Proposta de desenho”: falta explicitar os testes adversariais obrigatórios para ambas as mutações: token A/sessão B rejeitado, mesmo token/sessão aceito, token malformado rejeitado genericamente, sessão expirada rejeitada e `DocumentRequest` cancelado entre emissão e mutação rejeitado. O código atual confirma que expiração e liveness já são rechecadas em `resolveSession()`, mas o novo vínculo precisa de cobertura própria.

3. MENOR — `round1-claude-proposal.md`, item 2: “antes de qualquer efeito colateral” não é literalmente correto, pois `resolveSession()` já consome o rate limiter antes da checagem do vínculo. Isso não compromete a segurança nem o fechamento do bug; apenas significa que uma tentativa com sessão válida e path incompatível consumirá quota.

Nenhum achado bloqueante. Comparar os hashes de selector com `===` é adequado: trata-se de identidade opaca do recurso, não de validação do secret. Ignorar o secret do path preserva corretamente a decisão aprovada de que a sessão é autossuficiente após sua emissão. As três rotas restantes autenticam diretamente o token completo do path e não apresentam a colisão cookie/path.
